<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For the derived <i>p</i>-completion of the sphere, the canonical weak chromatic splitting map has no homotopy retraction at height <i>p</i> for every prime <i>p</i> ≥ 5, and at height $`p+1`$ for every prime <i>p</i> ≥ 7. The classical sphere product $`\beta_1^{(p-1)^2}`$ gives a nonzero kernel class in both ranges.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-height-p-kernel)): For every prime $p\geq5$, the image of $z_p$ in $\pi_{d_pk_p}L_{p-1}X$ is nonzero and is killed by $\pi_{d_pk_p}(i_{p,X})$. Consequently $i_{p,X}$ has no homotopy retraction.
  - Explanation: A named sphere product survives lower localization and vanishes under the canonical upper unit, obstructing a retraction.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-height-plus-one-kernel)): For every prime $p\geq7$, the image of the same $z_p$ in $\pi_{d_pk_p}L_pX$ is nonzero and is killed by $\pi_{d_pk_p}(i_{p+1,X})$. Consequently $i_{p+1,X}$ has no homotopy retraction.
  - Explanation: The same named sphere product obstructs a retraction at the next upper height in the stated prime range.
- **C3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-five)): At $p=5$, the image of $\beta_1^{16}$ in $\pi_{608}L_4X$ is nonzero and belongs to the kernel of $\pi_{608}(i_{5,X})$.
  - Explanation: The prime-five specialization identifies the explicit nonzero kernel class.
- **C4** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-exponents)): For every prime $p\geq5$, with $m=p^2+2$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{exp}}_H(E_{p-1})>m, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{exp}}_H\bigl(T(B_p)\bigr)\leq m.$$ A homotopy retraction of $i_{p,X}$ would make $E_{p-1}$ an $H$-equivariant retract of $T(B_p)$. Hence $i_{p,X}$ has no homotopy retraction, independently of Theorem <a href="#thm:height-p-kernel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:height-p-kernel">1</a>.
  - Explanation: Incompatible equivariant descent exponents give an independent obstruction to the canonical retraction.
- **C5** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-completion)): The completion map $c:S\to X=S_p^\wedge$ is a $K(a)$-equivalence for every integer $a\geq1$. If $1\leq t\leq r$ and $Y$ is $K(t)$-local, every map $S\to Y$ factors through $S\to X\to L_rX$.
  - Explanation: Positive-height localization cannot distinguish the sphere from its derived completion; local detectors consequently factor through the completed lower localization.
- **C6** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-kernel-criterion)): Let $n\geq2$, let $1\leq t\leq n-1$, and let $z\in\pi_qS$. Suppose a map $S\to Y$ to a $K(t)$-local spectrum carries $z$ to a nonzero element, and the unit $S\to L_{K(n)}S$ carries $z$ to zero. Then the image $\bar z\in\pi_qL_{n-1}X$ is nonzero and $\pi_q(i_{n,X})(\bar z)=0$.
  - Explanation: Lower detection and upper unit vanishing produce a kernel for the precise canonical map after completion.
- **C7** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-lower-sphere-product)): The image of $z_p$ is nonzero in each of $$\pi_{d(p-1)^2}L_{K(p-1)}S,\qquad \pi_{d(p-1)^2}L_{K(p-1)}X,\qquad \pi_{d(p-1)^2}L_rX\quad(r\geq p-1).$$ All three images are induced by the sphere unit and, for the completed input, by the completion map $S\to X$.
  - Explanation: The specified sphere product is detected by the lower localization, including for the completed input.
- **C8** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-lower-bar-product)): Let a finite group $Q$ act coherently on a commutative ring spectrum $R$. The ordinary homotopy fixed point spectral sequence has $$E_2^{s,t}=H^s(Q;\pi_tR)$$ and has the cup product on $E_2$ and the derivation rule for its differentials. Whenever this spectral sequence converges to $\pi_*R^{hQ}$ with a separated homotopy filtration whose successive quotients are $E_\infty$, its product on $E_\infty$ is the associated-graded product for that actual homotopy filtration.
  - Explanation: The ordinary fixed-point spectral sequence respects multiplication; separated convergence identifies its final product with the actual homotopy filtration product.
- **C9** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-lower-power)): The unit $\eta:S\to Y$ carries $z_p=\beta_1^{h^2}$ to a nonzero class in $\pi_{dh^2}Y$.
  - Explanation: A nonzero associated-graded power detects the actual named sphere product under the ring unit.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-semilinear)): Let a finite group $Q$ act on a commutative ring $W$. Suppose $w\in W$ satisfies $\sum_{g\in Q}g(w)=1$. For any semilinear $W$-module $P$, one has $H^i(Q,P)=0$ for $i>0$, with no finiteness or flatness hypothesis on $P$. In particular this holds for $W=W(\mathbb F_{p^a})$ and $Q=\mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(\mathbb F_{p^a}/\mathbb F_p)$, without a prime-to-$p$ hypothesis on $a$.
  - Explanation: A trace-one element contracts positive-degree cochains of every semilinear module without requiring a prime-to-prime group order.
- **C11** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-adic)): Let $G=S_0\rtimes Q$ be profinite with $Q$ finite, and let $D\geq0$ be an integer such that $\mathop{\mathrm{cd}}_p(S_0)\leq D$ for discrete $p$-primary modules. Let $\{M_j\}_j$ be a countable tower of finite discrete $p$-primary $G$-modules with surjective transitions, and give $M=\varprojlim_j M_j$ the inverse-limit topology. Suppose each $M_j$ is a $W$-module, $S_0$ acts $W$-linearly, $Q$ acts semilinearly, and $W$ admits the trace-one element of Lemma <a href="#lem:semilinear" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:semilinear">10</a>. Then $$H^s_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}(G,M_j)=0\quad(s>D),\qquad H^s_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}(G,M)=0\quad(s>D+1).$$ The second conclusion also holds if the first displayed finite-quotient vanishing is supplied directly, without a semidirect-product hypothesis.
  - Explanation: Finite-quotient vanishing passes to the adic inverse limit with the possible extra inverse-limit degree retained.
- **C12** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-cohomology)): Let $V_0$ be a finite ordinary $p$-local spectrum, let $DV_0=F(V_0,S)$, and give $M=\pi_q(E_a\wedge DV_0)$ its $\mathfrak m_a$-adic topology and Morava action. Then $$H^s_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}(\mathbb G_a,M)=0\qquad(s>a^2+1)$$ in each of the following cases: $a=p$ with $p\geq5$, and $a=p+1$ with $p\geq7$.
  - Explanation: The continuous cohomological bound applies to coefficients from every finite ordinary test, including torsion and both parities.
- **C14** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-adams-stage-images)): Two such resolutions of $V$ have the same stage-$s$ image in $[W,V]$ for every object $W$ and every $s\geq0$.
  - Explanation: Relative Adams resolutions have identical stage images, without asserting equivalence of their entire towers.
- **C15** (theorem, [source](#stmt-wimg-descent)): The strongly convergent descent spectral sequence $$\label{wimg:morava-page} E_2^{s,q}=H^s_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}(\mathbb G_a;(E_a)_q) \Longrightarrow\pi_{q-s}B_a$$ has the separated homotopy filtration $$\label{wimg:actual-filtration} F^s\pi_*B_a =\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\bigl(\pi_*I_a^{\otimes_a s} \xrightarrow{(\epsilon_a^{\otimes_a s})_*}\pi_*B_a\bigr), \qquad I_a^{\otimes_a0}=B_a.$$ Its successive quotients are $E_\infty^{s,*}$ with the usual total degree, and $F^rF^s\subseteq F^{r+s}$.
  - Explanation: The descent filtration is the actual image of tensor powers of the unit fiber, and it is separated and multiplicative.
- **C16** (bound, [source](#stmt-wimg-upper-filtration)): Let $a=p$ with $p\geq5$, or $a=p+1$ with $p\geq7$. Then $$\label{wimg:zero-tail} F^s\pi_*B_a=0\qquad(s\geq a^2+2).$$
  - Explanation: The cohomological vanishing line and separated convergence force the upper filtration tail to vanish.
- **C17** (theorem, [source](#stmt-wimg-upper-power)): For every $x\in\pi_dS$, the unit image of $x^k$ is zero in $\pi_{dk}B_p$ when $p\geq5$. When $p\geq7$, the image of the same product is also zero in $\pi_{dk}B_{p+1}$.
  - Explanation: Torsion and parity put sphere images in sufficiently high multiplicative filtration to annihilate the stated power.
- **C18** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cube)): For $s\geq0$ there is a natural equivalence of arrows over $\mathrm{id}_V$ in $\mathcal C_{/V}$: $$\begin{aligned} &\left(\mathop{\mathrm{fib}}\bigl(V\to\mathop{\mathrm{Tot}}_s C_A^\bullet(V)\bigr)\longrightarrow V\right)\\ &\qquad\simeq\left(J^{\otimes(s+1)}\otimes V \xrightarrow{\epsilon^{\otimes(s+1)}\otimes\mathrm{id}_V}V\right). \end{aligned}$$ The transition on the fibers from stage $s+1$ to stage $s$ applies $\epsilon$ to the final $J$-factor. Any exact functor $\Phi:\mathcal C\to\mathrm{Sp}$ preserves this finite fiber sequence.
  - Explanation: Finite Amitsur stages have tensor-power fibers with compatible transition maps; exact functors preserve these finite fiber sequences.
- **C19** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-exponent-calculus)): An $A$-module has exponent at most one. Bounds are preserved by retracts and by tensoring with arbitrary objects. For a cofiber sequence $U\to V\to W$, bounds $b$ and $c$ on $U$ and $W$ give bound $b+c$ on $V$. If $e_m(V)$ is null, $V$ is a retract of an object with $m$ successive layers $A\otimes J^{\otimes i}\otimes V$, $0\leq i<m$.
  - Explanation: Descent bounds survive tensoring and retracts, add across cofiber sequences, and provide finite algebra-module layers.
- **C20** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-pages)): For any exact $\Phi:\mathcal C\to\mathrm{Sp}$ and integer $b\geq1$:* 1. *If $V$ has $A$-exponent at most $b$, then $E_{b+1}^{s,q}=0$ for all $s\geq b$ and $q$.* 2. *If $V$ has some finite $A$-exponent and $E_2^{s,q}=0$ for all $s\geq b$ and $q$, then $\pi_d\Phi(e_b(V))=0$ for every $d$.* *The second assertion is zero tested image, not nullity of a map.
  - Explanation: An exponent bound forces a finite-page vanishing line; conversely a cohomological line and some finite exponent kill tested images without asserting map nullity.
- **C21** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-lower-hm)): For every odd prime $p$, put $t=p-1$ and let $E_t$ be Morava $E$-theory for the height-$t$ Honda formal group over $k_t=\mathbb F_{p^t}$, with its coherent stabilizer action. The pure stabilizer $\mathbb S_t$ contains a finite subgroup $H\cong C_p\rtimes C_{t^2}$ for which $$\label{eq:lower-algebra} \begin{gathered} \widehat H^*(H;(E_t)_*) =k_t[\alpha,\beta^{\pm1},\Delta^{\pm1}]/(\alpha^2),\\ \|\alpha\|=(1,2t),\qquad \|\beta\|=(2,2pt),\qquad \|\Delta\|=(0,2pt^2). \end{gathered}$$ The ordinary-to-Tate comparison is multiplicative and identifies $H^s(H;(E_t)_q)$ with the displayed Tate group for every $s>0$ and $q$.
  - Explanation: The pure stabilizer subgroup has the displayed Tate cohomology algebra, with multiplicative ordinary comparison in positive filtration.
- **C22** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-lower-exponent)): The $H$-exponent of $E_t$ is greater than $m$.
  - Explanation: A class survives the specified finite page above the proposed exponent bound, ruling that bound out.
- **C23** (theorem, [source](#stmt-wup-cochains)): For $r\geq0$, let $$Z_r=L_{K(a)}(E^{\wedge(r+1)}).$$ For every finite ordinary $p$-local spectrum $W$, there are natural graded isomorphisms $$\label{wup:tested-functions} \pi_*(Z_r\wedge W) \cong C_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}(\mathbb G_a^r,\pi_*(E\wedge W)).$$ The first $E$-factor acts by constant coefficients. The two-factor evaluation is multiplication after $1\wedge g$. In the cumulative coordinates $1,g_1,g_1g_2,\ldots,g_1\cdots g_r$ on the factors, these isomorphisms identify every coface and codegeneracy of the unit Amitsur object with the continuous inhomogeneous group-cochain operator.* *Consequently, apply the exact ordinary functor $F(V_0,-)$, for a finite ordinary $p$-local spectrum $V_0$, to the full unit Amitsur tower for $E_a$ in $\mathcal C_a$. In the page convention of Proposition <a href="#prop:pages" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:pages">20</a>, its second page is $$\label{wup:cochain-page} E_2^{s,q} =H^s_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}\bigl(\mathbb G_a,\pi_q(E_a\wedge D V_0)\bigr), \qquad D V_0=F(V_0,S).$$ Here $H^s_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}$ means the cohomology of continuous cochains with the adic topology on the indicated coefficient module.
  - Explanation: Actual finite-test cooperations identify every Amitsur coface and codegeneracy with continuous group-cochain operators.
- **C24** (theorem, [source](#stmt-wup-mixed-flat)): For positive heights $a,b$, the ordinary smash product has even homotopy $$\pi_*(E_a\wedge E_b)\cong (E_a)_*\otimes_{BP_*}BP_*BP\otimes_{BP_*}(E_b)_*.$$ It is flat over $(E_a)_*$ through the first factor.
  - Explanation: Ordinary mixed cooperations are even and flat through the first coefficient action.
- **C25** (theorem, [source](#stmt-wup-base-change)): Let $D_0$ be an ordinary right $E$-module for which $\pi_*D_0\otimes_{O_*}(-)$ is exact on finitely generated graded $O_*$-modules. The natural comparison $$\label{wup:relative-base-change} \pi_*D_0\otimes_{O_*}\pi_*(E\wedge W) \longrightarrow \pi_*(D_0\wedge W)$$ is an isomorphism for every ordinary $p$-local spectrum $W$.* *For every profinite set $T$, the functor $C_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}(T,-)$ is exact on finitely generated $O$-modules with their $\mathfrak m$-adic topologies. For every finitely generated graded $O_*$-module $M_*$, the natural map is an isomorphism $$\label{wup:function-tensor} C_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}(T,O_*)\otimes_{O_*}M_* \xrightarrow{\ \cong\ } C_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}(T,M_*).$$ The functions and the displayed graded isomorphism are understood degreewise.
  - Explanation: Exact base change computes ordinary smash homotopy, while continuous functions preserve exactness for finitely generated adic modules.
- **C26** (theorem, [source](#stmt-wup-completion-reduction)): Let $N$ be an ordinary $E$-module with flat graded homotopy $U_*=\pi_*N$. Its actual $K(a)$-localization map induces $$U_*\longrightarrow\pi_*L_{K(a)}N \cong\widehat{U_*}:=\varprojlim_j U_*/\mathfrak m^jU_*.$$ For a flat $O$-module $U$ and every $j\geq1$, $$\widehat U/\mathfrak m^j\widehat U\cong U/\mathfrak m^jU.$$ Both assertions apply degreewise to the periodic graded modules above.
  - Explanation: Flat ordinary homotopy completes under localization, and completion retains the stated reductions.
- **C27** (bound, [source](#stmt-wup-upper-null)): Let $p\geq5$ be prime and put $h=p$. In $\mathcal C_h$, let $I_h=\mathop{\mathrm{fib}}(B_h\to E_h)$. The actual map $$e_m(B_h):I_h^{\otimes_h m}\longrightarrow B_h, \qquad m=h^2+2=p^2+2,$$ is null. Thus $\mathop{\mathrm{exp}}_{E_h}(B_h)\leq m$, and $B_h$ is a retract of an object with $m$ successive layers $$E_h\otimes_h I_h^{\otimes_h i},\qquad 0\leq i<m.$$
  - Explanation: The specified tensor-power fiber map is genuinely null and supplies a retract with finite algebra-module layers.
- **C28** (theorem, [source](#stmt-wup-phantom)): Let $a\geq1$. An ordinary map $f:U\to B_a$ of $p$-local spectra is null if its precomposition with every map from a finite ordinary $p$-local spectrum to $U$ is null.
  - Explanation: Every map into a local sphere is determined by finite ordinary precompositions, so an ordinary phantom into that target is null.
- **C29** (bound, [source](#stmt-wup-completed-trace)): The mixed $H$-algebra $D'$ has an underlying degree-zero class whose $H$-trace is a unit. Every $D'$-module in $\mathcal D_H$ therefore has $H$-exponent at most one.
  - Explanation: The completed mixed algebra has a unit trace, forcing its equivariant modules to have the stated small exponent.
- **C30** (bound, [source](#stmt-wup-trace-split)): Let $D'$ be a commutative algebra in $\mathcal D_H$. Suppose an underlying class $z\in\pi_0D'$ has unit trace $$v=\sum_{g\in H}g(z)\in(\pi_0D')^\times.$$ Then every $D'$-module $U$ in $\mathcal D_H$ has $\mathop{\mathrm{exp}}_H(U)\leq1$.
  - Explanation: An underlying class with unit trace produces a coherent equivariant splitting without needing a fixed-point lift of that class.
- **C31** (theorem, [source](#stmt-wup-field-points)): For every unital ring map $x:R\to\Omega$ to a field, the equality $x\circ g=x$ for $g\in H$ implies $g=1$.
  - Explanation: Comparing formal-group isomorphisms shows the subgroup acts freely on actual field-valued points.
- **C32** (theorem, [source](#stmt-wup-algebraic-trace)): Let a finite group $H$ act on a commutative ring $R$. Suppose that for every unital field-valued map $x:R\to\Omega$, $x\circ g=x$ implies $g=1$. Then $$1\in\operatorname{im}\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}:R\to R^H\bigr), \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(r)=\sum_{g\in H}g(r).$$
  - Explanation: Freeness on field-valued maps forces the algebraic trace ideal to contain the unit.
- **C33** (bound, [source](#stmt-wup-transfer)): Let $p\geq5$ be prime, with $t=p-1$ and $h=p$. One has $$\mathop{\mathrm{exp}}_H\bigl(T(B_h)\bigr)\leq m=p^2+2.$$
  - Explanation: Transported upper layers become modules over the completed mixed algebra; their exponent bounds add through the finite filtration.
- **C34** (theorem, [source](#stmt-wup-contradiction)): Let $p\geq5$ be prime, put $t=p-1$ and $h=p$, and let $X=S^\wedge_p$ be the derived $p$-completed sphere. The canonical map $$i_{h,X}=L_t(\eta_X^{(h)}): L_tX\longrightarrow L_tL_{K(h)}X$$ has no homotopy retraction in ordinary spectra.
  - Explanation: A canonical retraction would transport to an equivariant retract and contradict the strict lower and weak upper exponent bounds.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-semilinear",
        "exact_quote": "Let a finite group $Q$ act on a commutative ring $W$. Suppose $w\\in W$ satisfies $\\sum_{g\\in Q}g(w)=1$. For any semilinear $W$-module $P$, one has $H^i(Q,P)=0$ for $i>0$, with no finiteness or flatness hypothesis on $P$. In particular this holds for $W=W(\\mathbb F_{p^a})$ and $Q=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gal}}(\\mathbb F_{p^a}/\\mathbb F_p)$, without a prime-to-$p$ hypothesis on $a$.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-318.5"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-semilinear",

          "source_id": "canonical:PM-318.5"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-adic",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G=S_0\\rtimes Q$ be profinite with $Q$ finite, and let $D\\geq0$ be an integer such that $\\mathop{\\mathrm{cd}}_p(S_0)\\leq D$ for discrete $p$-primary modules. Let $\\{M_j\\}_j$ be a countable tower of finite discrete $p$-primary $G$-modules with surjective transitions, and give $M=\\varprojlim_j M_j$ the inverse-limit topology. Suppose each $M_j$ is a $W$-module, $S_0$ acts $W$-linearly, $Q$ acts semilinearly, and $W$ admits the trace-one element of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:semilinear\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:semilinear\">10</a>. Then $$H^s_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{cts}}}(G,M_j)=0\\quad(s>D),\\qquad\n H^s_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{cts}}}(G,M)=0\\quad(s>D+1).$$ The second conclusion also holds if the first displayed finite-quotient vanishing is supplied directly, without a semidirect-product hypothesis.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-318.5"
      },
      "evidence": [

Then $$H^s_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{cts}}}(G,M_j)=0\\quad(s>D),\\qquad\n H^s_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{cts}}}(G,M)=0\\quad(s>D+1).$$ The second conclusion also holds if the first displayed finite-quotient vanishing is supplied directly, without a semidirect-product hypothesis.",

        {
          "anchor": "wimg:local-tower",
          "exact_quote": "In a stable symmetric monoidal $\\infty$-category with exact tensor, let $E$ be a unital algebra. Call an object $E$-injective if it is a retract of $E\\otimes Z$ for some $Z$. A relative $E$-Adams resolution of $V$ consists of triangles $$T_{s+1}\\longrightarrow T_s\\xrightarrow{q_s}Q_s,\n \\qquad T_0=V,$$ where each $Q_s$ is $E$-injective and $E\\otimes q_s$ is split monic.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-318.5"
        }
      ],
      "id": "C14",

          "source_id": "canonical:PM-318.5"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-adams-stage-images"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-wimg-descent",
        "exact_quote": "The strongly convergent descent spectral sequence $$\\label{wimg:morava-page}\n E_2^{s,q}=H^s_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{cts}}}(\\mathbb G_a;(E_a)_q)\n \\Longrightarrow\\pi_{q-s}B_a$$ has the separated homotopy filtration $$\\label{wimg:actual-filtration}\n F^s\\pi_*B_a\n =\\mathop{\\mathrm{im}}\\bigl(\\pi_*I_a^{\\otimes_a s}\n       \\xrightarrow{(\\epsilon_a^{\\otimes_a s})_*}\\pi_*B_a\\bigr),\n \\qquad I_a^{\\otimes_a0}=B_a.$$ Its successive quotients are $E_\\infty^{s,*}$ with the usual total degree, and $F^rF^s\\subseteq F^{r+s}$.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-318.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-wimg-descent",

          "source_id": "canonical:PM-318.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The strongly convergent descent spectral sequence $$\\label{wimg:morava-page}\n E_2^{s,q}=H^s_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{cts}}}(\\mathbb G_a;(E_a)_q)\n \\Longrightarrow\\pi_{q-s}B_a$$ has the separated homotopy filtration $$\\label{wimg:actual-filtration}\n F^s\\pi_*B_a\n =\\mathop{\\mathrm{im}}\\bigl(\\pi_*I_a^{\\otimes_a s}\n       \\xrightarrow{(\\epsilon_a^{\\otimes_a s})_*}\\pi_*B_a\\bigr),\n \\qquad I_a^{\\otimes_a0}=B_a.$$ Its successive quotients are $E_\\infty^{s,*}$ with the usual total degree, and $F^rF^s\\subseteq F^{r+s}$.",

      "exact_statement": "For $s\\geq0$ there is a natural equivalence of arrows over $\\mathrm{id}_V$ in $\\mathcal C_{/V}$: $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\left(\\mathop{\\mathrm{fib}}\\bigl(V\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tot}}_s C_A^\\bullet(V)\\bigr)\\longrightarrow V\\right)\\\\\n &\\qquad\\simeq\\left(J^{\\otimes(s+1)}\\otimes V\n \\xrightarrow{\\epsilon^{\\otimes(s+1)}\\otimes\\mathrm{id}_V}V\\right).\n \\end{aligned}$$ The transition on the fibers from stage $s+1$ to stage $s$ applies $\\epsilon$ to the final $J$-factor.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-exponent-calculus",
        "exact_quote": "An $A$-module has exponent at most one. Bounds are preserved by retracts and by tensoring with arbitrary objects. For a cofiber sequence $U\\to V\\to W$, bounds $b$ and $c$ on $U$ and $W$ give bound $b+c$ on $V$. If $e_m(V)$ is null, $V$ is a retract of an object with $m$ successive layers $A\\otimes J^{\\otimes i}\\otimes V$, $0\\leq i<m$.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-318.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-pages",
        "exact_quote": "For any exact $\\Phi:\\mathcal C\\to\\mathrm{Sp}$ and integer $b\\geq1$:*\n\n1.  *If $V$ has $A$-exponent at most $b$, then $E_{b+1}^{s,q}=0$ for all $s\\geq b$ and $q$.*\n\n2.  *If $V$ has some finite $A$-exponent and $E_2^{s,q}=0$ for all $s\\geq b$ and $q$, then $\\pi_d\\Phi(e_b(V))=0$ for every $d$.*\n\n*The second assertion is zero tested image, not nullity of a map.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-318.5"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "canonical:PM-318.5"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-lower-hm",
        "exact_quote": "For every odd prime $p$, put $t=p-1$ and let $E_t$ be Morava $E$-theory for the height-$t$ Honda formal group over $k_t=\\mathbb F_{p^t}$, with its coherent stabilizer action. The pure stabilizer $\\mathbb S_t$ contains a finite subgroup $H\\cong C_p\\rtimes C_{t^2}$ for which $$\\label{eq:lower-algebra}\n \\begin{gathered}\n \\widehat H^*(H;(E_t)_*)\n =k_t[\\alpha,\\beta^{\\pm1},\\Delta^{\\pm1}]/(\\alpha^2),\\\\\n |\\alpha|=(1,2t),\\qquad |\\beta|=(2,2pt),\\qquad\n |\\Delta|=(0,2pt^2).\n \\end{gathered}$$ The ordinary-to-Tate comparison is multiplicative and identifies $H^s(H;(E_t)_q)$ with the displayed Tate group for every $s>0$ and $q$.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-318.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-lower-hm",
          "exact_quote": "For every odd prime $p$, put $t=p-1$ and let $E_t$ be Morava $E$-theory for the height-$t$ Honda formal group over $k_t=\\mathbb F_{p^t}$, with its coherent stabilizer action.           "source_id": "canonical:PM-318.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every odd prime $p$, put $t=p-1$ and let $E_t$ be Morava $E$-theory for the height-$t$ Honda formal group over $k_t=\\mathbb F_{p^t}$, with its coherent stabilizer action.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-wup-cochains",
        "exact_quote": "For $r\\geq0$, let $$Z_r=L_{K(a)}(E^{\\wedge(r+1)}).$$ For every finite ordinary $p$-local spectrum $W$, there are natural graded isomorphisms $$\\label{wup:tested-functions}\n \\pi_*(Z_r\\wedge W)\n \\cong C_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{cts}}}(\\mathbb G_a^r,\\pi_*(E\\wedge W)).$$ The first $E$-factor acts by constant coefficients. The two-factor evaluation is multiplication after $1\\wedge g$. In the cumulative coordinates $1,g_1,g_1g_2,\\ldots,g_1\\cdots g_r$ on the factors, these isomorphisms identify every coface and codegeneracy of the unit Amitsur object with the continuous inhomogeneous group-cochain operator.*",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-318.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-wup-cochains",
          "exact_quote": "For $r\\geq0$, let $$Z_r=L_{K(a)}(E^{\\wedge(r+1)}).$$ For every finite ordinary $p$-local spectrum $W$, there are natural graded isomorphisms $$\\label{wup:tested-functions}\n \\pi_*(Z_r\\wedge W)\n \\cong C_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{cts}}}(\\mathbb G_a^r,\\pi_*(E\\wedge W)).$$ The first $E$-factor acts by constant coefficients. The two-factor evaluation is multiplication after $1\\wedge g$. In the cumulative coordinates $1,g_1,g_1g_2,\\ldots,g_1\\cdots g_r$ on the factors, these isomorphisms identify every coface and codegeneracy of the unit Amitsur object with the continuous inhomogeneous group-cochain operator.*",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-318.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For $r\\geq0$, let $$Z_r=L_{K(a)}(E^{\\wedge(r+1)}).$$ For every finite ordinary $p$-local spectrum $W$, there are natural graded isomorphisms $$\\label{wup:tested-functions}\n \\pi_*(Z_r\\wedge W)\n \\cong C_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{cts}}}(\\mathbb G_a^r,\\pi_*(E\\wedge W)).$$ The first $E$-factor acts by constant coefficients. The two-factor evaluation is multiplication after $1\\wedge g$. In the cumulative coordinates $1,g_1,g_1g_2,\\ldots,g_1\\cdots g_r$ on the factors, these isomorphisms identify every coface and codegeneracy of the unit Amitsur object with the continuous inhomogeneous group-cochain operator.*\n\n\n*Consequently, apply the exact ordinary functor $F(V_0,-)$, for a finite ordinary $p$-local spectrum $V_0$, to the full unit Amitsur tower for $E_a$ in $\\mathcal C_a$. In the page convention of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:pages\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:pages\">20</a>, its second page is $$\\label{wup:cochain-page}\n E_2^{s,q}\n =H^s_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{cts}}}\\bigl(\\mathbb G_a,\\pi_q(E_a\\wedge D V_0)\\bigr),\n \\qquad D V_0=F(V_0,S).$$ Here $H^s_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{cts}}}$ means the cohomology of continuous cochains with the adic topology on the indicated coefficient module.",
      "explanation": "Actual finite-test cooperations identify every Amitsur coface and codegeneracy with continuous group-cochain operators.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-wup-cochains",
          "exact_quote": "For $r\\geq0$, let $$Z_r=L_{K(a)}(E^{\\wedge(r+1)}).$$ For every finite ordinary $p$-local spectrum $W$, there are natural graded isomorphisms $$\\label{wup:tested-functions}\n \\pi_*(Z_r\\wedge W)\n \\cong C_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{cts}}}(\\mathbb G_a^r,\\pi_*(E\\wedge W)).$$ The first $E$-factor acts by constant coefficients.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-318.5"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "sec:cooperations",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a positive height $a$, and write $$\\begin{gathered}\n E=E_a,\\qquad k_a=\\mathbb F_{p^a},\\qquad\n O=O_a=W(k_a)[[u_1,\\ldots,u_{a-1}]],\\\\\n O_*=(E_a)_*=O[u^{\\pm1}],\\quad |u|=-2,\\qquad\n \\mathfrak m=\\mathfrak m_a=(p,u_1,\\ldots,u_{a-1}).\n \\end{gathered}$$",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-318.5"
        }

          "source_id": "canonical:PM-318.5"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-wup-base-change",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D_0$ be an ordinary right $E$-module for which $\\pi_*D_0\\otimes_{O_*}(-)$ is exact on finitely generated graded $O_*$-modules.         "source_id": "canonical:PM-318.5"

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-wup-base-change",
          "exact_quote": "Let $D_0$ be an ordinary right $E$-module for which $\\pi_*D_0\\otimes_{O_*}(-)$ is exact on finitely generated graded $O_*$-modules.       "exact_statement": "Let $D_0$ be an ordinary right $E$-module for which $\\pi_*D_0\\otimes_{O_*}(-)$ is exact on finitely generated graded $O_*$-modules. For every finitely generated graded $O_*$-module $M_*$, the natural map is an isomorphism $$\\label{wup:function-tensor}\n C_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{cts}}}(T,O_*)\\otimes_{O_*}M_*\n \\xrightarrow{\\ \\cong\\ } C_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{cts}}}(T,M_*).$$ The functions and the displayed graded isomorphism are understood degreewise.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-wup-completion-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $N$ be an ordinary $E$-module with flat graded homotopy $U_*=\\pi_*N$. Its actual $K(a)$-localization map induces $$U_*\\longrightarrow\\pi_*L_{K(a)}N\n \\cong\\widehat{U_*}:=\\varprojlim_j U_*/\\mathfrak m^jU_*.$$ For a flat $O$-module $U$ and every $j\\geq1$, $$\\widehat U/\\mathfrak m^j\\widehat U\\cong U/\\mathfrak m^jU.$$ Both assertions apply degreewise to the periodic graded modules above.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-318.5"
      },
      "evidence": [

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Morava E-theory and local sphere** ([source](#par:common-notation)): Let $E_a$ be Morava $E$-theory of the height-$a$ Honda formal group over $\mathbb F_{p^a}$, and put $B_a=L_{K(a)}S$.
- **Localization unit** ([source](#eq:weak-unit)): For a spectrum $Z$, let $\eta_Z^{(n)}:Z\to L_{K(n)}Z$ be the localization unit.
- **Canonical weak splitting map** ([source](#eq:weak-unit)): We study $$\label{eq:weak-unit} i_{n,X}=L_{n-1}(\eta_X^{(n)}): L_{n-1}X\longrightarrow L_{n-1}L_{K(n)}X.$$
- **Homotopy retraction** ([source](#eq:weak-unit)): A retraction is a map of spectra $r$ with $r i_{n,X}\simeq\mathrm{id}$; it need not be natural in the finite input.
- **Named beta sphere product** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For an odd prime, let $$\beta_1\in\pi_{d_p}S,\qquad d_p=2p(p-1)-2,\qquad k_p=(p-1)^2, \qquad z_p=\beta_1^{k_p},$$ where $\beta_1$ is the classical first beta element.
- **Equivariant descent exponent** ([source](#pm-h-4)): Define $\mathop{\mathrm{exp}}_H(V)$ as the least $b\geq1$ such that $J_H^{\wedge b}\wedge V\to V$ is null, and as infinity if no such $b$ exists.
- **Cross-height transport** ([source](#pm-h-4)): Set $$T(V)=L_{K(t)}(E_t\wedge V),$$ with $H$ acting on the first factor.
- **Algebra exponent bound** ([source](#sec:exponent-pages)): For $b\geq1$ say that $V$ has $A$-exponent at most $b$ if the actual map $$e_b(V)=\epsilon^{\otimes b}\otimes\mathrm{id}_V: J^{\otimes b}\otimes V\longrightarrow V$$ is null.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R-completion** [claim](#stmt-lem-completion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-completion): Identify the completion fiber and show its positive-height acyclicity. -> Local detecting maps factor through the completed lower localization.
- **R-lower** [claim](#stmt-prop-lower-power) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-lower-power): Multiply the actual detected unit image in the separated ordinary filtration. -> The named sphere product has a nonzero lower detector.
- **R-cohomology** [claim](#stmt-prop-cohomology) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-cohomology): Exclude pure subgroup prime torsion, apply dimension bounds and semilinear descent, then retain the inverse-limit degree. -> Common upper continuous-cohomology bound.
- **R-upper** [claim](#stmt-wimg-upper-power) / [proof](#proof-stmt-wimg-upper-power): Identify actual Adams images, use torsion and parity, and compare the multiplicative power with the separated zero tail. -> The same named sphere product vanishes at both stipulated upper heights.
- **R-kernel** [claim](#stmt-thm-height-p-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-wimg-upper-power-2): Apply completion factorization and the naturality squares of the kernel criterion. -> Nonzero canonical kernel and failure of a retraction.
- **R-kernel-next** [claim](#stmt-thm-height-plus-one-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-wimg-upper-power-2): Apply the same kernel criterion at the next upper height in its narrower prime range. -> The same product obstructs the next-height canonical retraction.
- **R-lower-exponent** [claim](#stmt-prop-lower-exponent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-lower-exponent): Exclude incoming differentials by degree and use the derivation rule to preserve a prime-divisible power on the finite page. -> Strict lower equivariant exponent bound.
- **R-upper-null** [claim](#stmt-wup-upper-null) / [proof](#proof-stmt-wup-phantom-2): Obtain some finite exponent before testing, kill every finite tested image, then use the local-sphere phantom criterion. -> Actual null map and finite upper layers.
- **R-trace** [claim](#stmt-wup-completed-trace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-wup-algebraic-trace-2): Use freeness on field-valued points to obtain algebraic trace one, lift through adic completion, and apply coherent splitting. -> Every completed mixed-algebra module has the stated exponent bound.
- **R-transport** [claim](#stmt-wup-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-wup-transfer): Transport individual ordinary module layers and add their equivariant exponent bounds. -> Upper exponent bound for the transported local sphere.
- **R-exponent-contradiction** [claim](#stmt-wup-contradiction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-wup-contradiction): Transport the actual canonical completion square and compare retract-invariant exponent bounds. -> Independent obstruction to the canonical retraction.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **Ikernel**: The named sphere product has a nonzero lower image killed by the canonical weak splitting map, obstructing a homotopy retraction.
- **Icut**: The half-cut formulas produce nonnegative barycentric vectors each summing to one.
- **Isupport**: The front and back supports lie in the faces sharing the cut coordinate.
- **Inaturality**: Half-cut vectors commute with barycentric pushforward along order-preserving maps.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **Afinite**: Treat finite-coordinate checks as a certificate of nonretraction. -> Barycentric arithmetic does not establish a nonzero kernel class in localized homotopy.
- **Anull**: Replace actual map nullity by zero tested homotopy images. -> The exponent requires an actual null map; tested-image vanishing alone is weaker.
- **Aadic**: Drop the extra inverse-limit degree from the common cohomology bound. -> A possible inverse-limit contribution remains at the boundary.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The ordinary-product proof gives explicit half-cut barycentric vectors and a finite coordinate inverse. This implements that finite geometric construction on rational coordinates; it does not certify homotopy groups, spectral-sequence convergence, or nonretraction.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON batch of nonempty lists of exact rational strings on stdin.
# PRECONDITIONS: Each list has nonnegative coordinates summing to one.
# OUTPUTS: JSON batch of pairs of exact rational barycentric vectors.
# TIME: Linear in the total coordinate count, counting exact rational operations.
# SPACE: Linear in the total coordinate count, including output.
# ARITHMETIC: Certified exact rational arithmetic with python-flint fmpq.
# FAILURE: Malformed JSON, empty vectors, negative coordinates or incorrect sums raise errors.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpq

def half_cut(row):
    coords = [fmpq(x) for x in row]
    if not coords or any(x < 0 for x in coords) or sum(coords) != 1:
        raise ValueError('invalid barycentric vector')
    midpoint = fmpq(1, 2)
    cursor = fmpq(0)
    left, right = [], []
    for mass in coords:
        end = cursor + mass
        left.append(2 * (min(end, midpoint) - min(cursor, midpoint)))
        right.append(2 * (max(end, midpoint) - max(cursor, midpoint)))
        cursor = end
    return [[str(x) for x in left], [str(x) for x in right]]

payload = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
print(json.dumps([half_cut(row) for row in payload]))
```

    "oracle_code": "import sys as system\nimport json as codec\nfrom fractions import Fraction as Rat\nfrom itertools import combinations_with_replacement as monotone_maps\n\ntry:\n    data = codec.load(open(system.argv[1]))\n    actual = codec.load(open(system.argv[2]))\n    if system.argv[3] != 'VALID' or len(actual) != len(data):\n        raise ValueError('bad expected marker or batch length')\n    table = {}\n    for row, pair in zip(data, actual):\n        weights = tuple(map(Rat, row))\n        if len(pair) != 2 or any(len(v) != len(weights) for v in pair):\n            raise ValueError('bad output shape')\n        lhs, rhs = [tuple(map(Rat, v)) for v in pair]\n        remaining = Rat(1, 2)\n        allocation = []\n        for weight in weights:\n            piece = weight if weight <= remaining else remaining\n            allocation.append(piece)\n            remaining -= piece\n        target_left = tuple(piece * 2 for piece in allocation)\n        target_right = tuple((weight-piece) * 2 for weight,piece in zip(weights,allocation))\n        if lhs != target_left or rhs != target_right:\n            raise ValueError('reservoir allocation mismatch')\n        if min(lhs+rhs) < 0 or sum(lhs) != 1 or sum(rhs) != 1:\n            raise ValueError('simplex violation')\n        if tuple((a+b)/2 for a,b in zip(lhs,rhs)) != weights:\n            raise ValueError('inverse mismatch')\n        accumulated = Rat(0)\n        cuts = []\n        for index,weight in enumerate(weights):\n            if accumulated <= Rat(1,2) <= accumulated+weight:\n                cuts.append(index)\n            accumulated += weight\n        for cut in cuts:\n            if any(lhs[i] for i in range(cut+1,len(weights))) or any(rhs[i] for i in range(cut)):\n                raise ValueError('front/back support violation')\n        table[weights] = (lhs,rhs)\n    for weights,(lhs,rhs) in table.items():\n        for size in range(1,5):\n            for mapping in monotone_maps(range(size),len(weights)):\n                push = lambda v: tuple(sum(v[i] for i,j in enumerate(mapping) if j==k) for k in range(size))\n                merged = push(weights)\n                if merged in table and table[merged] != (push(lhs),push(rhs)):\n                    raise ValueError('naturality violation')\nexcept Exception:\n    system.exit(7)\nsystem.exit(0)\n",
    "output_contract": "Exact rational front and back half-cut barycentric vectors, normalized and ordered as in the source formulas.",
    "reason": "The ordinary-product proof gives explicit half-cut barycentric vectors and a finite coordinate inverse. This implements that finite geometric construction on rational coordinates; it does not certify homotopy groups, spectral-sequence convergence, or nonretraction.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON batch of nonempty lists of exact rational strings on stdin.\n# PRECONDITIONS: Each list has nonnegative coordinates summing to one.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON batch of pairs of exact rational barycentric vectors.\n# TIME: Linear in the total coordinate count, counting exact rational operations.\n# SPACE: Linear in the total coordinate count, including output.\n# ARITHMETIC: Certified exact rational arithmetic with python-flint fmpq.\n# FAILURE: Malformed JSON, empty vectors, negative coordinates or incorrect sums raise errors.\nimport json, sys\nfrom flint import fmpq\n\ndef half_cut(row):\n    coords = [fmpq(x) for x in row]\n    if not coords or any(x < 0 for x in coords) or sum(coords) != 1:\n        raise ValueError('invalid barycentric vector')\n    midpoint = fmpq(1, 2)\n    cursor = fmpq(0)\n    left, right = [], []\n    for mass in coords:\n        end = cursor + mass\n        left.append(2 * (min(end, midpoint) - min(cursor, midpoint)))\n        right.append(2 * (max(end, midpoint) - max(cursor, midpoint)))\n        cursor = end\n    return [[str(x) for x in left], [str(x) for x in right]]\n\npayload = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())\nprint(json.dumps([half_cut(row) for row in payload]))\n",

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result concerns homotopy retractions of localized spectra; it supplies no software interface or executable representation of those spectra. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The obstruction is a nonzero sphere class killed by a chromatic localization map, with no game state, simulation dynamics, or sampling contract. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proof gives mathematical criteria for nonretraction but no effective procedure for constructing and testing the required spectra. |
| algorithms | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | The finite adic inverse is an explicit geometric sum. It can be evaluated conditionally when quotient-ring arithmetic and a truncation are represented effectively; the paper does not supply such a representation. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The towers and filtrations are objects of stable homotopy theory; the paper gives no concrete storage representation or update contract. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proof works with spectra and exact categorical constructions, with no hardware execution model or acceleration contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The kernel class obstructs a retraction of spectra, with no cryptographic adversary, security reduction, or computational hardness statement. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The theorem concerns chromatic localization and supplies no learning objective, statistical model, or training procedure. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The descent exponent bounds nullity of a tensor-power map, with no objective function, feasible search space, or optimisation procedure. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The coherent group actions and retractions occur in categories of spectra, with no communication, fault, or distributed execution model. |
| other (chromatic homotopy theory) | True | strong | inspirational | inferred | The named lower-detected sphere product obstructs the canonical weak chromatic splitting retraction. The independent descent-exponent argument supplies a second mathematical obstruction. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (counterexample_scope): The counterexample uses the derived completion of the sphere and the canonical weak chromatic splitting map. Blocked use: ['Do not infer that every map between chromatic overlap spectra fails to split.', 'Do not infer nonretraction for every completed finite input.']
- **L2** (hypothesis): The height-$p$ kernel theorem assumes prime $p\geq5$; the height-$p+1$ kernel theorem assumes prime $p\geq7$. Blocked use: ['Do not extend these kernel results to smaller primes or other heights.']
- **L3** (formal_scope): A zero tested image is weaker than nullity of an actual map. The upper exponent argument requires its separate nullity proof. Blocked use: ['Do not certify an exponent bound solely from vanishing on tested homotopy groups.']
- **L4** (edge_case): The adic cohomology bound retains the possible extra inverse-limit degree. Blocked use: ['Do not remove the inverse-limit contribution without an additional vanishing argument.']
- **L5** (formal_scope): The sharper sphere-coefficient line does not supply the same line for arbitrary finite-test coefficients or identify an actual image filtration. Blocked use: ['Do not substitute the sharper sphere bound into the finite-test exponent argument.']
- **L6** (hypothesis): The finite-page tested-image criterion assumes an existing finite exponent. Blocked use: ['Do not derive nullity or a finite exponent from a cohomological vanishing line alone.']
- **L7** (formal_scope): The relative Adams comparison proves equality of finite-stage images. Blocked use: ['Do not replace equality of stage images by an equivalence of towers.']
- **L8** (formal_scope): The lower exponent argument uses survival on a finite spectral-sequence page. Blocked use: ['Do not infer a permanent cycle or a sphere representative from this finite-page obstruction.']
- **L9** (nonconstructive): A finite adic truncation level does not ensure a finite quotient set. Effective ring representations and arithmetic are additional requirements for computation. Blocked use: ['Do not exhaustively enumerate the mixed quotient ring solely because the truncation level is finite.', 'Do not claim an effective construction of a mixed trace element from the existence proof.']
- **L10** (hypothesis): Trace-one contraction applies to semilinear modules over a ring with the stated trace-one element. Blocked use: ['Do not replace semilinearity and trace one by an assumption about group order alone.']
- **L11** (formal_scope): Freeness requires equality of actual field-valued maps. Blocked use: ['Do not replace equality of field-valued maps by invariance of their kernels.']
- **L12** (formal_scope): Transport preserves the finite layers using ordinary module structures; preservation of the upper localized tensor product is not required. Blocked use: ['Do not claim the transport functor preserves the upper localized tensor product.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For the derived <i>p</i>-completion of the sphere, the canonical weak chromatic splitting map has no homotopy retraction at height <i>p</i> for every prime <i>p</i> ≥ 5, and at height $`p+1`$ for every prime <i>p</i> ≥ 7. The classical sphere product $`\beta_1^{(p-1)^2}`$ gives a nonzero kernel class in both ranges.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### The canonical weak splitting map

<a id="eq:weak-unit"></a>
Fix a prime $p$ and work in ordinary $p$-local spectra, with sphere $S=S^0_{(p)}$. Let $K(a)$ denote Morava $K$-theory of height $a$, put $K(0)=H\mathbb Q$, and write $$L_r=L_{K(0)\vee\cdots\vee K(r)},\qquad
 X=S_p^\wedge=\operatorname{holim}_{j\geq1}S/p^j.$$ Thus $L_r$ is Johnson–Wilson $E(r)$-localization. For a spectrum $Z$, let $\eta_Z^{(n)}:Z\to L_{K(n)}Z$ be the localization unit. We study $$\label{eq:weak-unit}
 i_{n,X}=L_{n-1}(\eta_X^{(n)}):
 L_{n-1}X\longrightarrow L_{n-1}L_{K(n)}X.$$ The weak chromatic splitting conjecture predicts a homotopy retraction of this map whenever $X$ is the derived $p$-completion of a finite spectrum. A retraction is a map of spectra $r$ with $r i_{n,X}\simeq\mathrm{id}$; it need not be natural in the finite input. We disprove this universal assertion with finite input the sphere.

For an odd prime, let $$\beta_1\in\pi_{d_p}S,\qquad
 d_p=2p(p-1)-2,\qquad k_p=(p-1)^2,
 \qquad z_p=\beta_1^{k_p},$$ where $\beta_1$ is the classical first beta element.

<a id="stmt-thm-height-p-kernel"></a>
<div id="thm:height-p-kernel" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (A named kernel at height $p$). *For every prime $p\geq5$, the image of $z_p$ in $\pi_{d_pk_p}L_{p-1}X$ is nonzero and is killed by $\pi_{d_pk_p}(i_{p,X})$. Consequently $i_{p,X}$ has no homotopy retraction.*

</div>

<a id="stmt-thm-height-plus-one-kernel"></a>
<div id="thm:height-plus-one-kernel" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (The same kernel at height $p+1$). *For every prime $p\geq7$, the image of the same $z_p$ in $\pi_{d_pk_p}L_pX$ is nonzero and is killed by $\pi_{d_pk_p}(i_{p+1,X})$. Consequently $i_{p+1,X}$ has no homotopy retraction.*

</div>

<a id="stmt-thm-five"></a>
<div id="thm:five" class="corollary">

**Corollary 3** (The prime-five class). *At $p=5$, the image of $\beta_1^{16}$ in $\pi_{608}L_4X$ is nonzero and belongs to the kernel of $\pi_{608}(i_{5,X})$.*

</div>

The proof detects $z_p$ under a height-$(p-1)$ unit and kills it under both upper units. The lower detection follows from the Hopkins–Miller calculation recorded by Heard (Heard 2015, proof of Theorem 5.6 and Lemma 5.8) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[heard2015](#ref-heard2015)]: the actual unit detects the named $\beta_1$, and its $k_p$th power remains nonzero in the ordinary homotopy fixed point spectral sequence. At the upper height $a$, we construct a separated multiplicative filtration with the unit image of $\beta_1$ in filtration at least two and $F^{a^2+2}=0$. The required inequalities are $$2k_p-(p^2+1)=p^2-4p+1>0\quad(p\geq5),$$ $$2k_p-((p+1)^2+1)=p(p-6)>0\quad(p\geq7).$$ Completion and naturality then place the nonzero lower image in the kernel of the exact map <a href="#eq:weak-unit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weak-unit">[eq:weak-unit]</a>. For example, at $p=7$ the class $\beta_1^{36}$ has degree $82\cdot36=2952$ and gives kernels at both heights seven and eight.

<a id="par:common-notation"></a>
###### Notation.

Let $E_a$ be Morava $E$-theory of the height-$a$ Honda formal group over $\mathbb F_{p^a}$, and put $B_a=L_{K(a)}S$. Unless a localized tensor is explicitly displayed, smash products and function spectra are ordinary. All homotopy groups are those of the underlying spectra.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### An independent descent-exponent obstruction

The height-$p$ nonretraction also follows from incompatible finite descent lengths. Put $t=p-1$, and let the finite pure-stabilizer subgroup $H=C_p\rtimes C_{(p-1)^2}$ act on $E_t$. In $\mathcal D_H=\mathop{\mathrm{Fun}}(BH,\mathrm{Sp}_{(p)})$, with pointwise ordinary smash, put $A_H=F(H_+,S)$ with its translation action and $J_H=\mathop{\mathrm{fib}}(S\to A_H)$. Define $\mathop{\mathrm{exp}}_H(V)$ as the least $b\geq1$ such that $J_H^{\wedge b}\wedge V\to V$ is null, and as infinity if no such $b$ exists. Set $$T(V)=L_{K(t)}(E_t\wedge V),$$ with $H$ acting on the first factor.

<a id="stmt-thm-exponents"></a>
<div id="thm:exponents" class="theorem">

**Theorem 4** (Incompatible descent exponents). *For every prime $p\geq5$, with $m=p^2+2$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{exp}}_H(E_{p-1})>m,
 \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{exp}}_H\bigl(T(B_p)\bigr)\leq m.$$ A homotopy retraction of $i_{p,X}$ would make $E_{p-1}$ an $H$-equivariant retract of $T(B_p)$. Hence $i_{p,X}$ has no homotopy retraction, independently of Theorem <a href="#thm:height-p-kernel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:height-p-kernel">1</a>.*

</div>

For the lower bound, a class survives to one specified finite page of the ordinary homotopy fixed point spectral sequence. No permanent cycle or sphere representative is needed. For the upper bound, Devinatz–Hopkins’ nilpotence theorem supplies a finite exponent before any homotopy test is chosen (Devinatz and Hopkins 2004, Appendix A, Proposition A.3) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[dh2004](#ref-dh2004)]. The numerical cochain bound then makes the relevant map zero after every finite ordinary test. A Brown–Comenetz argument proves that the map itself is null, and a coherent trace construction transports the resulting finite filtration to height $p-1$. The finite-stage formalism used here belongs to the nilpotence and descent theory of Mathew and Mathew–Naumann–Noel (Mathew 2016; Mathew, Naumann, and Noel 2017, 2019) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[mathew2016](#ref-mathew2016), [mnn2017](#ref-mnn2017), [mnn2019](#ref-mnn2019)].

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Relation to earlier splitting results

Hopkins’ chromatic splitting conjecture, recorded by Hovey (Hovey 1995, Conjecture 4.2, especially part (v)) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[hovey1995](#ref-hovey1995)], predicts a specific decomposition of chromatic overlap as well as the weak unit retraction. These predictions differ already at $n=p=2$. Beaudry disproves the proposed strong summand formula (Beaudry 2017, Theorem 1.0.4) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[beaudry2017](#ref-beaudry2017)]; Beaudry–Goerss–Henn give a revised decomposition with Moore terms that retains the canonical unit as a retract (Beaudry, Goerss, and Henn 2022, Theorem 1.1.6) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[bgh2022](#ref-bgh2022)]. Their Conjectures 1.1.10–1.1.11 distinguish the strong and completed-finite-input weak formulations, and their introduction records the weak form at all primes through height two. The theorem numbers refer to the versioned texts identified in the bibliography. Outside finite inputs, Minami reports Devinatz’s counterexample for a $BP$ analogue allowing completed $BP$ (Minami 2003, Introduction and Remark 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[minami2003](#ref-minami2003)]. The present counterexamples use the completion of the finite sphere.

Long beta powers in the sphere were known earlier. Lee–Ravenel prove $\beta_1^{p^2-p-1}\ne0$ for $p\geq7$ and report $\beta_1^{17}\ne0$, $\beta_1^{18}=0$ at five (Lee and Ravenel 1994, opening theorem and introduction) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[lee-ravenel1994](#ref-lee-ravenel1994)]. Ravenel’s first-beta detection calculation relates the cobar class to stabilizer cohomology (Ravenel 1978, Theorem 4 and its proof in Section 2) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[ravenel1978](#ref-ravenel1978)]. The additional requirement here is detection after a specified lower localization and vanishing of that same named sphere product under the upper unit.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
###### Organization.

Sections <a href="#sec:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:completion">2</a>–<a href="#sec:kernels" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:kernels">5</a> prove the named kernel results. Their upper bound uses dimension at height $p+1$ and a semilinear trace-one contraction at height $p$, retaining the possible inverse-limit degree in both cases. Sections <a href="#sec:towers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:towers">6</a>–<a href="#sec:exponent-transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:exponent-transport">11</a> give the independent exponent proof. Appendix <a href="#sec:ordinary-products" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:ordinary-products">12</a> proves the ordinary filtered-product lemma used by the lower detector and by the finite-page argument.

<a id="sec:completion"></a>
### Completion and the canonical map

The lower detectors are local spectra, whereas the weak splitting problem uses a completed finite input. The following argument connects these settings without interchanging smash products and inverse limits.

<a id="stmt-lem-completion"></a>
<div id="lem:completion" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5**. *The completion map $c:S\to X=S_p^\wedge$ is a $K(a)$-equivalence for every integer $a\geq1$. If $1\leq t\leq r$ and $Y$ is $K(t)$-local, every map $S\to Y$ factors through $S\to X\to L_rX$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-completion"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-completion</summary>

*Proof.* Take the inverse limit of the cofiber sequences $S\xrightarrow{p^j}S\to S/p^j$, with multiplication by $p$ on the left spheres and identity on the middle spheres. The fiber of $c$ is $$C\simeq\operatorname{holim}(\cdots\xrightarrow pS\xrightarrow pS)
   \simeq F(S[1/p],S).$$ Multiplication by $p$ is an equivalence on this function spectrum, because it is an equivalence on its first argument. It remains an equivalence after ordinary smash with $K(a)$. But $\pi_*(K(a)\wedge C)$ is a module over $K(a)_*$ and is annihilated by $p$. Thus it is zero, proving the first claim.

A map to a $K(t)$-local target consequently extends across $c$. The target is also $L_r$-local when $t\leq r$, since an $L_r$-acyclic spectrum is $K(t)$-acyclic. The extension therefore factors through $L_rX$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-prop-kernel-criterion"></a>
<div id="prop:kernel-criterion" class="proposition">

**Proposition 6** (An actual sphere kernel). *Let $n\geq2$, let $1\leq t\leq n-1$, and let $z\in\pi_qS$. Suppose a map $S\to Y$ to a $K(t)$-local spectrum carries $z$ to a nonzero element, and the unit $S\to L_{K(n)}S$ carries $z$ to zero. Then the image $\bar z\in\pi_qL_{n-1}X$ is nonzero and $\pi_q(i_{n,X})(\bar z)=0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-kernel-criterion"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-kernel-criterion</summary>

*Proof.* Lemma <a href="#lem:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:completion">5</a> factors the detecting map through $L_{n-1}X$, so $\bar z$ is nonzero. Write $\eta$ for $K(n)$-localization and $\lambda$ for $L_{n-1}$-localization. The relevant two squares are $$\begin{CD}
 S @>{c}>> X\\
 @V{\eta_S}VV @VV{\eta_X}V\\
 L_{K(n)}S @>{L_{K(n)}c}>> L_{K(n)}X
\end{CD}
\qquad
\begin{CD}
 X @>{\eta_X}>> L_{K(n)}X\\
 @V{\lambda_X}VV @VV{\lambda_{L_{K(n)}X}}V\\
 L_{n-1}X @>{i_{n,X}}>> L_{n-1}L_{K(n)}X.
\end{CD}$$ Both commute by naturality of localization units. Equivalently, $$\eta_Xc\simeq L_{K(n)}(c)\eta_S,
 \qquad i_{n,X}\lambda_X\simeq\lambda_{L_{K(n)}X}\eta_X.$$ The first sends $z$ to zero, and the second then gives the asserted kernel of the exact map <a href="#eq:weak-unit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weak-unit">[eq:weak-unit]</a>. A retraction would induce a left inverse on this homotopy group and is therefore impossible. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:lower-sphere-product"></a>
### The named sphere product at the lower height

The classical first beta element supplies the lower detector at every odd prime. The fixed-point calculation identifies its image under an actual unit, and the ordinary multiplicative filtration then detects its long power. This gives one named sphere product for both upper heights.

Fix an odd prime $p$ and put $$h=p-1,\qquad d=2p(p-1)-2,\qquad
 z_p=\beta_1^{(p-1)^2},\qquad \beta_1\in\pi_dS.$$

<a id="stmt-thm-lower-sphere-product"></a>
<div id="thm:lower-sphere-product" class="theorem">

**Theorem 7** (The lower sphere product). *The image of $z_p$ is nonzero in each of $$\pi_{d(p-1)^2}L_{K(p-1)}S,\qquad
 \pi_{d(p-1)^2}L_{K(p-1)}X,\qquad
 \pi_{d(p-1)^2}L_rX\quad(r\geq p-1).$$ All three images are induced by the sphere unit and, for the completed input, by the completion map $S\to X$.*

</div>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### The actual unit in the ordinary detector

Let $E_h$ be Morava $E$-theory over $\mathbb F_{p^h}$. Write $\mathbb S_h$ for the pure stabilizer and $\mathbb G_h=\mathbb S_h\rtimes\mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(\mathbb F_{p^h}/\mathbb F_p)$ for the extended stabilizer. Choose the finite subgroup $G\subset\mathbb G_h$ whose intersection with the pure stabilizer is $C_p\rtimes C_{h^2}$ and whose Galois projection is onto, as in (Heard 2015, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[heard2015](#ref-heard2015)]. Put $$Y=E_h^{hG},\qquad \eta:S\longrightarrow Y.$$ Here $Y$ is the ordinary homotopy fixed point spectrum and $\eta$ is its unit as a commutative ring spectrum. Devinatz–Hopkins identify the ordinary finite-group fixed-point spectral sequence with the local Adams spectral sequence and give strong convergence (Devinatz and Hopkins 2004, Theorems 2(ii) and 3) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[dh2004](#ref-dh2004)]. Its decreasing homotopy filtration $F^s\pi_*Y$ is therefore separated, with $$F^s\pi_qY/F^{s+1}\pi_qY\cong E_\infty^{s,q+s}.$$

<a id="eq:lower-unit-detection"></a>
<a id="eq:lower-even-infinity"></a>
The Hopkins–Miller calculation gives the positive-filtration part of its $E_2$ page as the corresponding part of $$\mathbb F_p[\alpha,\beta,\Delta^{\pm1}]/(\alpha^2),\qquad
 |\alpha|=(1,2h),\quad |\beta|=(2,2ph),\quad
 |\Delta|=(0,2ph^2);$$ see (Heard 2015, Proposition 5.5) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[heard2015](#ref-heard2015)] and (Heard, Mathew, and Stojanoska 2017, Proposition 2.7) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[hms2017](#ref-hms2017)]. We need two further assertions of that calculation. First, Heard identifies $\beta$ with the image of the named stable $\beta_1$ under $\eta$ (Heard 2015, proof of Theorem 5.6) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[heard2015](#ref-heard2015)]. Allowing for the choice of cohomology generator, this says $$\label{eq:lower-unit-detection}
 \eta_*(\beta_1)\in F^2\pi_dY,\qquad
 [\eta_*(\beta_1)]=c\beta\in E_\infty^{2,2ph},
 \qquad c\in\mathbb F_p^\times.$$ The brackets denote the class modulo $F^3$. The Ext-to-group-cohomology comparison and permanence are also recorded in the discussion after (Heard, Mathew, and Stojanoska 2017, Proposition 2.7) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[hms2017](#ref-hms2017)]. Second, in positive even filtration Heard’s ordinary $E_\infty$ calculation is $$\label{eq:lower-even-infinity}
 E_\infty^{\mathrm{even},*}
   \cong \mathbb F_p[\Delta^{\pm p}][\beta]/(\beta^{h^2+1}),
 \qquad \text{in positive filtration},$$ by (Heard 2015, Lemma 5.8) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[heard2015](#ref-heard2015)]. In particular $\beta^{h^2}$ is nonzero. These inputs hold for every odd prime; the named detection is not restricted to $p=5$. They concern the extended group $G$ and $\mathbb F_p$ coefficients. The pure subgroup and its larger coefficient field will be used separately in the exponent argument.

<a id="stmt-lem-lower-bar-product"></a>
<div id="lem:lower-bar-product" class="lemma">

**Lemma 8** (The ordinary filtered product). *Let a finite group $Q$ act coherently on a commutative ring spectrum $R$. The ordinary homotopy fixed point spectral sequence has $$E_2^{s,t}=H^s(Q;\pi_tR)$$ and has the cup product on $E_2$ and the derivation rule for its differentials. Whenever this spectral sequence converges to $\pi_*R^{hQ}$ with a separated homotopy filtration whose successive quotients are $E_\infty$, its product on $E_\infty$ is the associated-graded product for that actual homotopy filtration.*

</div>

The proof is given in Appendix <a href="#sec:ordinary-products" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:ordinary-products">12</a>.

<a id="stmt-prop-lower-power"></a>
<div id="prop:lower-power" class="proposition">

**Proposition 9** (The actual product in the detector). *The unit $\eta:S\to Y$ carries $z_p=\beta_1^{h^2}$ to a nonzero class in $\pi_{dh^2}Y$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-lower-power"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-lower-power</summary>

*Proof.* Apply Lemma <a href="#lem:lower-bar-product" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:lower-bar-product">8</a> to the ordinary spectral sequence just described. The class of $\eta_*(\beta_1)^{h^2}$ in $F^{2h^2}\pi_{dh^2}Y/F^{2h^2+1}\pi_{dh^2}Y$ is $$c^{h^2}\beta^{h^2}\ne0$$ by <a href="#eq:lower-unit-detection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lower-unit-detection">[eq:lower-unit-detection]</a> and <a href="#eq:lower-even-infinity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lower-even-infinity">[eq:lower-even-infinity]</a>. Hence the homotopy class itself is nonzero. Since $\eta$ is a ring map, this power is exactly $\eta_*(\beta_1^{h^2})$, the image of the specified sphere product. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-lower-power-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-lower-power</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:lower-sphere-product" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:lower-sphere-product">7</a>.* The spectrum $Y$ is $K(h)$-local, since $E_h$ is local and local objects are closed under homotopy limits. Its unit therefore factors through $L_{K(h)}S$, where the image of $z_p$ must be nonzero by Proposition <a href="#prop:lower-power" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:lower-power">9</a>. Lemma <a href="#lem:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:completion">5</a> makes $L_{K(h)}(S\to X)$ an equivalence; naturality carries that nonzero image to $L_{K(h)}X$. For every $r\geq h$, the same lemma factors the detecting unit through $S\to X\to L_rX\to Y$. Its nonzero value on $z_p$ proves the remaining assertion. ◻

</details>

At $p=5$ this is the named class $\beta_1^{16}$ in degree $38\cdot16=608$. Section <a href="#sec:kernels" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:kernels">5</a> kills its image at height five as part of the uniform upper argument.

<a id="sec:adic"></a>
### Semilinear descent and an adic bound

At height $a=p$ for $p\geq5$, and at height $a=p+1$ for $p\geq7$, the upper arguments need continuous cohomology to vanish above degree $a^2+1$, for both sphere coefficients and finite-test coefficients. We first bound the cohomology of finite adic quotients and then pass to their countable inverse limit. At height $p$ the Galois quotient has order $p$, and its semilinear action on Witt coefficients supplies the required descent. At height $p+1$ the full extended group has finite $p$-cohomological dimension.

Put $k_a=\mathbb F_{p^a}$ and write $$(E_a)_*=O_a[u^{\pm1}],\quad
 O_a=W(k_a)[[u_1,\ldots,u_{a-1}]],\quad
 \mathfrak m_a=(p,u_1,\ldots,u_{a-1}),\quad |u|=-2,$$ and $\mathbb G_a=\mathbb S_a\rtimes\mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(k_a/\mathbb F_p)$. Here $\mathbb S_a$ is the pure stabilizer over $k_a$.

Exactness of Galois invariants for $p$-complete twisted Witt modules is proved in (Goerss and Hopkins 2020, Lemma 1.14) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[goerss-hopkins2020](#ref-goerss-hopkins2020)]. The explicit contraction below applies to arbitrary semilinear modules.

<a id="stmt-lem-semilinear"></a>
<div id="lem:semilinear" class="lemma">

**Lemma 10** (Trace-one contraction). *Let a finite group $Q$ act on a commutative ring $W$. Suppose $w\in W$ satisfies $\sum_{g\in Q}g(w)=1$. For any semilinear $W$-module $P$, one has $H^i(Q,P)=0$ for $i>0$, with no finiteness or flatness hypothesis on $P$. In particular this holds for $W=W(\mathbb F_{p^a})$ and $Q=\mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(\mathbb F_{p^a}/\mathbb F_p)$, without a prime-to-$p$ hypothesis on $a$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-semilinear"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-semilinear</summary>

*Proof.* Use homogeneous group cochains: a degree-$q$ cochain is an equivariant function $f:Q^{q+1}\to P$, with differential the alternating deletion of coordinates. For $q\geq1$ define $$(hf)(g_0,\ldots,g_{q-1})
   =\sum_{\gamma\in Q}\gamma(w)f(\gamma,g_0,\ldots,g_{q-1}).$$ For $g\in Q$, replacing $\gamma$ by $g\gamma$ proves that $hf$ is equivariant: semilinearity converts $(g\gamma)(w)\,g f$ into $g(\gamma(w)f)$. In $hd+dh$, the terms deleting one of the displayed $g_i$ cancel in pairs. The term deleting the inserted coordinate is $\sum_\gamma\gamma(w)f=f$. Thus $hd+dh=\mathrm{id}$ in positive degrees, proving acyclicity for arbitrary $P$.

For the Witt-ring case, the finite-field trace is onto. Lift a residue trace-one element to $w_0\in W$. Its Witt trace lies in $\mathbb Z_p$ and is congruent to one modulo $p$, hence is a unit. Dividing $w_0$ by this invariant unit supplies $w$. We have not divided by $|Q|$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-adic"></a>
<div id="lem:adic" class="lemma">

**Lemma 11** (Finite quotients and the extra adic degree). *Let $G=S_0\rtimes Q$ be profinite with $Q$ finite, and let $D\geq0$ be an integer such that $\mathop{\mathrm{cd}}_p(S_0)\leq D$ for discrete $p$-primary modules. Let $\{M_j\}_j$ be a countable tower of finite discrete $p$-primary $G$-modules with surjective transitions, and give $M=\varprojlim_j M_j$ the inverse-limit topology. Suppose each $M_j$ is a $W$-module, $S_0$ acts $W$-linearly, $Q$ acts semilinearly, and $W$ admits the trace-one element of Lemma <a href="#lem:semilinear" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:semilinear">10</a>. Then $$H^s_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}(G,M_j)=0\quad(s>D),\qquad
 H^s_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}(G,M)=0\quad(s>D+1).$$ The second conclusion also holds if the first displayed finite-quotient vanishing is supplied directly, without a semidirect-product hypothesis.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-adic"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-adic</summary>

<a id="eq:adic-milnor"></a>
*Proof.* The pure-subgroup cochain complex is $W$-linear and has semilinear $Q$-action, including the conjugation action on its arguments: for an inhomogeneous $b$-cochain, $$(\gamma f)(s_1,\ldots,s_b)=
 \gamma\bigl(f(\gamma^{-1}s_1\gamma,\ldots,
                  \gamma^{-1}s_b\gamma)\bigr).$$ The pure differential is $W$-linear and commutes with this action. Its cohomology is therefore semilinear, whether or not that cohomology has separately been shown finite. The continuous Hochschild–Serre sequence at the discrete coefficient level is $$H^i\bigl(Q,H^b_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}(S_0,M_j)\bigr)
 \Longrightarrow H^{i+b}_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}(G,M_j).$$ Lemma <a href="#lem:semilinear" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:semilinear">10</a> kills its positive $Q$-columns, giving $$H^s_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}(G,M_j)=H^s_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}(S_0,M_j)^Q.$$ This proves the first bound. Continuous cochains into $M$ are compatible cochains into its finite quotients. Their transition maps are degreewise surjective: a continuous cochain has a finite value set, and any choice of lifts of those values gives a continuous cochain into the next finite quotient. No equivariant section is needed for inhomogeneous cochains. The countable inverse-limit sequence is therefore $$\label{eq:adic-milnor}
 0\longrightarrow\varprojlim_j{}^1 H^{s-1}_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}(G,M_j)
 \longrightarrow H^s_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}(G,M)
 \longrightarrow\varprojlim_j H^s_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}(G,M_j)\longrightarrow0.$$ Both outer terms vanish for $s>D+1$. At the remaining boundary degree, the same sequence gives $$H^{D+1}_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}(G,M)
 \cong\varprojlim_j{}^1 H^D_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}(G,M_j).$$ Thus the bound retains the possible extra inverse-limit degree. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-prop-cohomology"></a>
<div id="prop:cohomology" class="proposition">

**Proposition 12** (The two upper heights). *Let $V_0$ be a finite ordinary $p$-local spectrum, let $DV_0=F(V_0,S)$, and give $M=\pi_q(E_a\wedge DV_0)$ its $\mathfrak m_a$-adic topology and Morava action. Then $$H^s_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}(\mathbb G_a,M)=0\qquad(s>a^2+1)$$ in each of the following cases: $a=p$ with $p\geq5$, and $a=p+1$ with $p\geq7$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-cohomology"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-cohomology</summary>

*Proof.* The pure stabilizer is the maximal-order unit group in the central division algebra over $\mathbb Q_p$ of dimension $a^2$, so it is compact $p$-adic analytic of that dimension (Morava 1985, secs. 2.1–2.2) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[morava1985](#ref-morava1985)]. It has no element $g$ of order $p$ in either stated range. Indeed, $(g-1)\Phi_p(g)=0$ in a division algebra and $g\ne1$ imply $\Phi_p(g)=0$. The polynomial $\Phi_p(X+1)$ is Eisenstein, so $g$ generates the degree-$(p-1)$ subfield $\mathbb Q_p(\zeta_p)$. The division algebra would then be a vector space over this subfield, forcing $p-1\mid a^2$. For $a=p$ this forces $p-1\mid1$; for $a=p+1$ it forces $p-1\mid4$. These are impossible in the stated ranges. The Lazard–Serre theorem therefore gives $\mathop{\mathrm{cd}}_p(\mathbb S_a)=a^2$ for discrete $p$-primary coefficients (Serre 1965, sec. 1, Corollary (1)) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[serre1965](#ref-serre1965)].

For each $q$, the coefficient module $M$ is finitely generated over the complete Noetherian ring $O_a$, by the finite construction of $DV_0$. It is complete and separated; its quotients $M_j=M/\mathfrak m_a^jM$ are finite discrete $p$-primary modules with surjective transitions. This uses no flatness of $M$ and allows torsion and both parities. The maximal ideal is invariant. For $V_0=S$, continuity of the action on these quotients is part of the Morava action used by Devinatz–Hopkins (Devinatz and Hopkins 2004, Theorem 1(ii) and Remark 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[dh2004](#ref-dh2004)]. For every finite $V_0$, the actual action and its continuity are also obtained by the ordinary cooperation construction of Proposition <a href="#wup:cochains" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="wup:cochains">23</a> below.

The pure stabilizer fixes $W(k_a)$. It fixes the residue field and thus the unique Hensel lifts of the roots of $X^{p^a}-X$, which are the Teichmüller representatives; continuity then fixes their convergent Witt expansions. Hence its action on $M_j$ is Witt-linear, while $Q=\mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(k_a/\mathbb F_p)$ acts semilinearly by the Witt lift. At $a=p$, Lemma <a href="#lem:adic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:adic">11</a> with $D=p^2$ gives the bound. The full group has the split Galois subgroup of order $p$; this conclusion is specific to the stated coefficients. The cohomological-dimension theorem was applied only to $\mathbb S_p$.

At $a=p+1$ with $p\geq7$, the Galois quotient has order $p+1$, prime to $p$. An order-$p$ element of $\mathbb G_a$ would therefore lie in $\mathbb S_a$, where we have just excluded it. The full group is a finite extension of the compact $p$-adic analytic pure group and has the same dimension $a^2$. The same dimension theorem now applies to the full group itself and gives $H^s_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}(\mathbb G_a,M_j)=0$ for $s>a^2$. Apply only the inverse-limit part of Lemma <a href="#lem:adic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:adic">11</a> to this finite-quotient bound. In both cases the finite quotients vanish for $s>a^2$, and <a href="#eq:adic-milnor" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:adic-milnor">[eq:adic-milnor]</a> gives the asserted bound $s>a^2+1$ for $M$. ◻

</details>

For the sphere-kernel argument, take $V_0=S$, so that $M=(E_a)_q$. The exponent argument uses the same bound for every finite $V_0$, including modules with torsion and both parities. In both applications the topology is the full maximal-ideal topology, as in the continuous-cochain convention of Devinatz–Hopkins (Devinatz and Hopkins 2004, Remark 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[dh2004](#ref-dh2004)].

<a id="stmt-rem-sphere-sharp"></a>
<div id="rem:sphere-sharp" class="remark">

*Remark 13* (A sharper line for sphere coefficients). For the sphere homotopy fixed point spectral sequence, Bobkova and collaborators state an $E_2$ vanishing line at $s=n^2+1$ when $p-1\nmid n$ (Bobkova et al. 2025, Equation (2) and Remark 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[bobkova2025](#ref-bobkova2025)]. At $p=n=5$ this gives $H^s_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}(\mathbb G_5;(E_5)_q)=0$ for $s>25$. That statement concerns the ordinary sphere coefficients $(E_n)_q$. It does not assert the same line for every finite-test module $\pi_q(E_n\wedge DV_0)$ or identify an actual image filtration. Proposition <a href="#prop:cohomology" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:cohomology">12</a> supplies the common conservative bound used in this paper.

</div>

<a id="sec:kernels"></a>
### Upper annihilation and the canonical kernels

At each upper height, torsion and parity place the unit image of $\beta_1$ in Adams filtration two. The cohomological bound of Section <a href="#sec:adic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:adic">4</a> makes the sufficiently high filtration zero. To apply this to the same power detected at the lower height, we identify the Adams filtration with images of tensor powers of the unit fiber. This also proves its multiplicativity directly.

<a id="wimg:local-tower"></a>
#### Relative Adams stages

We first isolate the finite-stage comparison. In a stable symmetric monoidal $\infty$-category with exact tensor, let $E$ be a unital algebra. Call an object $E$-injective if it is a retract of $E\otimes Z$ for some $Z$. A relative $E$-Adams resolution of $V$ consists of triangles $$T_{s+1}\longrightarrow T_s\xrightarrow{q_s}Q_s,
 \qquad T_0=V,$$ where each $Q_s$ is $E$-injective and $E\otimes q_s$ is split monic. All stages map to $V$ by their composites down the tower. This is the relative convention of (Devinatz and Hopkins 2004, Appendix A) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[dh2004](#ref-dh2004)].

<a id="stmt-lem-adams-stage-images"></a>
<div id="lem:adams-stage-images" class="lemma">

**Lemma 14** (Equality of relative Adams-stage images). *Two such resolutions of $V$ have the same stage-$s$ image in $[W,V]$ for every object $W$ and every $s\geq0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-adams-stage-images"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-adams-stage-images</summary>

*Proof.* Suppose a map $f_s:T_s\to T'_s$ over $V$ has been constructed, and put $g_s=q'_sf_s$. Choose a retract $$Q'_s\xrightarrow{\iota_s}E\otimes Z_s
       \xrightarrow{\rho_s}Q'_s,\qquad \rho_s\iota_s=1,$$ and a splitting $\sigma_s:E\otimes Q_s\to E\otimes T_s$ with $\sigma_s(1_E\otimes q_s)=1$. The composite <a id="wimg:extension"></a>$$\label{wimg:extension}
\begin{aligned}
 \overline g_s:\quad Q_s
 &\xrightarrow{\eta_{Q_s}}E\otimes Q_s
 \xrightarrow{\sigma_s}E\otimes T_s\\
 &\xrightarrow{1_E\otimes(\iota_sg_s)}E\otimes E\otimes Z_s
 \xrightarrow{\mu\otimes1}E\otimes Z_s
 \xrightarrow{\rho_s}Q'_s
\end{aligned}$$ extends $g_s$ along $q_s$. Indeed, unit naturality gives $\eta_{Q_s}q_s=(1_E\otimes q_s)\eta_{T_s}$. The splitting removes $1_E\otimes q_s$, and a second use of unit naturality followed by the module unit law gives $$\overline g_s q_s
 =\rho_s(\mu\otimes1)(1_E\otimes(\iota_sg_s))\eta_{T_s}
 =\rho_s\iota_sg_s=g_s.$$ Complete this square to a map of fiber triangles to obtain $f_{s+1}:T_{s+1}\to T'_{s+1}$. Starting with $f_0=\mathrm{id}_V$ gives maps over $V$ at every finite stage, and hence one inclusion between the stage-$s$ images in $[W,V]$. Repeating the construction in the other direction proves equality. The comparison begins at stage zero on $V$, so the indices agree. Only these finite-stage maps are used; no equivalence of towers is asserted. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-13"></a>
#### The separated upper filtration

For a positive height $a$, work in the local category $\mathcal C_a=\mathrm{Sp}_{K(a)}$, with $$B_a=L_{K(a)}S,\qquad
 U\otimes_a V=L_{K(a)}(U\wedge V),\qquad
 I_a=\mathop{\mathrm{fib}}(B_a\to E_a),\qquad \epsilon_a:I_a\to B_a.$$ The tensor unit is $B_a$. These local tensors will be used to describe homotopy classes of the underlying ordinary spectra.

<a id="stmt-wimg-descent"></a>
<div id="wimg:descent" class="lemma">

<a id="wimg:morava-page"></a>
<a id="wimg:actual-filtration"></a>
**Lemma 15** (The actual Adams filtration). *The strongly convergent descent spectral sequence $$\label{wimg:morava-page}
 E_2^{s,q}=H^s_{\mathop{\mathrm{cts}}}(\mathbb G_a;(E_a)_q)
 \Longrightarrow\pi_{q-s}B_a$$ has the separated homotopy filtration $$\label{wimg:actual-filtration}
 F^s\pi_*B_a
 =\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\bigl(\pi_*I_a^{\otimes_a s}
       \xrightarrow{(\epsilon_a^{\otimes_a s})_*}\pi_*B_a\bigr),
 \qquad I_a^{\otimes_a0}=B_a.$$ Its successive quotients are $E_\infty^{s,*}$ with the usual total degree, and $F^rF^s\subseteq F^{r+s}$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-wimg-descent"><summary>Proof of stmt-wimg-descent</summary>

*Proof.* Devinatz–Hopkins give <a href="#wimg:morava-page" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="wimg:morava-page">[wimg:morava-page]</a> with strong convergence and identify it with the local $E_a$-Adams spectral sequence (Devinatz and Hopkins 2004, Theorem 1(iii)–(iv)) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[dh2004](#ref-dh2004)]; the full extended group is open in itself. The coefficient topology is the full maximal-ideal-adic topology of (Devinatz and Hopkins 2004, Remark 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[dh2004](#ref-dh2004)]. Their ideal $(p,v_1,\ldots,v_{a-1})$ agrees degreewise with $\mathfrak m_a=(p,u_1,\ldots,u_{a-1})$, since $v_i=u_i u^{1-p^i}$ and $u$ is invertible.

The tensor powers $T_s=I_a^{\otimes_a s}$ form resolution triangles <a id="wimg:power-triangles"></a>$$\label{wimg:power-triangles}
 T_{s+1}\longrightarrow T_s
     \xrightarrow{q_s}E_a\otimes_a T_s.$$ Their right terms are $E_a$-injective, and multiplication on $E_a$ retracts $E_a\otimes_a q_s$.
